     1	//! Expand a planned view into source-table lanes before collecting indexes.
     2	use super::{intent, intent_walk as walk};
     3	use super::catalog::Catalog;
     4	use crate::ast::*;
     5	use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, type_error};
     6	use std::collections::BTreeMap;
     7	
     8	pub(super) fn expand(catalog: &mut Catalog, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
     9	    let mut views = BTreeMap::new();
    10	    for view in catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| &doc.views) {
    11	        if views.insert(view.name.clone(), view.clone()).is_some() || catalog.tables.contains_key(&view.name) {
    12	            diagnostics.push(type_error(&view.site, format!("duplicate view {}", view.name), "give each table and view a distinct name"));
    13	        }
    14	    }
    15	    for doc in &mut catalog.documents {
    16	        for view in &doc.views {
    17	            let site = view.site.clone();
    18	            doc.programs.push(ProgramDecl { delivery: None, held: false, predicts: false, server: false, native: false,
    19	                kind: ProgramKind::Query, name: format!("$view:{}", view.name), args: Vec::new(), prelude: Vec::new(), site: site.clone(),
    20	                body: vec![Stmt::Return { site: site.clone(), value: Expr::Read(ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None,
    21	                    table: view.name.clone(), keys: Vec::new(), seeks: Vec::new(), computed: BTreeMap::new(), index: None, order_keys: vec!["id".into()],
    22	                    filter: None, matching: None, range: Some(RangeSpec { order: Order::Asc, take: 1, cursor: None, site: site.clone() }), site,
    23	                }) }],
    24	            });
    25	        }
    26	        walk::document(doc, &mut |value| {
    27	            if let Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } = value {
    28	                catalog.read_origins.entry(read.site.to_string()).or_insert_with(|| catalog.source.expression(&Expr::Read(read.clone())));
    29	                catalog.canonical_read_origins.entry(read.site.to_string()).or_insert_with(|| if read.index.is_none() { crate::print::read_expr(read) } else { catalog.source.expression(&Expr::Read(read.clone())) });
    30	            }
    31	        });
    32	        walk::document(doc, &mut |value| {
    33	            if let Expr::Exists { read, site } = value && let Some(view) = views.get(&read.table) {
    34	                let mut read = read.clone();
    35	                read.range = Some(RangeSpec { order: Order::Asc, take: 1, cursor: None, site: site.clone() });
    36	                match read_view(view, &read) {
    37	                    Ok(merge) => *value = Expr::Binary { op: ">".into(), left: Box::new(Expr::Call { name: "count".into(), args: vec![Expr::Merge(merge)], site: site.clone() }),
    38	                        right: Box::new(Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Number("0".into()), site: site.clone() }), site: site.clone() },
    39	                    Err(error) => diagnostics.push(error),
    40	                }
    41	            } else if let Expr::Read(read) = value && let Some(view) = views.get(&read.table) {
    42	                match read_view(view, read) { Ok(merge) => *value = Expr::Merge(merge), Err(error) => diagnostics.push(error) }
    43	            }
    44	        });
    45	    }
    46	}
    47	
    48	fn read_view(view: &ViewDecl, read: &ReadExpr) -> Fallible<MergeExpr> {
    49	    if !read.keys.is_empty() || read.index.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() {
    50	        return Err(type_error(&read.site, "view reads use predicates and projected order fields", format!("{} where .id = value first 20 by .id", view.name)).guide("reads"));
    51	    }
    52	    let range = read.range.clone().unwrap_or(RangeSpec { order: Order::Asc, take: 8192, cursor: None, site: read.site.clone() });
    53	    let mut lanes = Vec::new();
    54	    let order = if read.order_keys.is_empty() { vec!["id".into()] } else { read.order_keys.clone() };
    55	    for (branch_number, branch) in view.branches.iter().enumerate() {
    56	        let mut projection = BTreeMap::from([("id".to_owned(), focal("id", &branch.site))]);
    57	        for field in &branch.fields {
    58	            match field {
    59	                ShapeField::Bare { name, site } => { projection.insert(name.clone(), focal(name, site)); }
    60	                ShapeField::Field { name, value, .. } if name != "id" => { projection.insert(name.clone(), value.clone()); }
    61	                _ => return Err(type_error(&branch.site, "view branches list named fields and carry their source id implicitly", "write { post: .id, actor: .authorId, at }; omit an explicit id field").guide("reads")),
    62	            }
    63	        }
    64	        let mut source_order = Vec::new();
    65	        for field in &order {
    66	            let Some(source) = projection.get(field).and_then(|value| intent::column(value)) else {
    67	                return Err(type_error(&branch.site, format!("view {} order field .{field} must project a bare column in branch {}", view.name, branch.source), format!("project {field}: .<sourceColumn> in every branch")).guide("reads"));
    68	            };
    69	            source_order.push(source.to_owned());
    70	        }
    71	        let mut filter = Vec::new();
    72	        if let Some(predicate) = &branch.predicate { filter.push(predicate.clone()); }
    73	        let mut eliminate = false;
    74	        if let Some(predicate) = &read.filter {
    75	            let mut predicates = Vec::new();
    76	            intent::split(*predicate.clone(), &mut predicates);
    77	            for mut predicate in predicates {
    78	                // Literal equality to a constant projection can prune a lane;
    79	                // dynamic equalities remain ordinary source-row predicates.
    80	                if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = &predicate && op == "=" {
    81	                    for (field, other) in [(left.as_ref(), right.as_ref()), (right.as_ref(), left.as_ref())] {
    82	                        if let Some(projected) = intent::column(field).and_then(|name| projection.get(name))
    83	                            && let (Expr::Literal { value: a, .. }, Expr::Literal { value: b, .. }) = (projected, other) && a != b { eliminate = true; }
    84	                    }
    85	                }
    86	                super::view_predicate::project(&mut predicate, &projection)?;
    87	                filter.push(predicate);
    88	            }
    89	        }
    90	        if eliminate { continue; }
    91	        let planned = ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), table: branch.source.clone(), keys: Vec::new(), seeks: Vec::new(), filter: intent::conjunction(filter), index: None,
    92	            order_keys: source_order, matching: read.matching.clone(), range: read.range.clone(), site: read.site.clone() };
    93	        let mut fields = branch.fields.clone();
    94	        if !fields.iter().any(|field| matches!(field, ShapeField::Bare { name, .. } if name == "id")) {
    95	            fields.push(ShapeField::Field { name: "id".into(), value: focal("id", &branch.site), site: branch.site.clone() });
    96	        }
    97	        lanes.push(MergeLane { admission: None, nulls: None, distinct: None, branch: Some(format!("{}:{branch_number}", view.name)), planned: Some(Box::new(planned)), project: Some(fields), table: Some(branch.source.clone()), prefix: Vec::new(), columns: None, loop_: None, site: branch.site.clone() });
    98	    }
    99	    Ok(MergeExpr { view_order: order, table: view.name.clone(), matching: None, filter: None, index: String::new(), order: range.order, take: range.take, cursor: range.cursor, lanes, site: read.site.clone() })
   100	}

     1	//! Conservative control outcomes, composed in execution order.
     2	use super::{Expr, Stmt, Value};
     3	
     4	const FALLS_THROUGH: u8 = 1;
     5	const BREAKS: u8 = 2;
     6	const CONTINUES: u8 = 4;
     7	// A refusal, like a return, cannot reach any later use of a local.
     8	const RETURNS: u8 = 8;
     9	
    10	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
    11	pub(super) struct Outcomes(u8);
    12	
    13	impl Outcomes {
    14	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.1 — loop transfers carry local types; later statements cannot erase those paths.
    15	    pub(super) fn block(statements: &[Stmt]) -> Self {
    16	        let mut outcomes = FALLS_THROUGH;
    17	        for statement in statements {
    18	            if outcomes & FALLS_THROUGH == 0 { break; }
    19	            // Only paths still falling through execute the next statement.
    20	            outcomes = (outcomes & !FALLS_THROUGH) | Self::statement(statement);
    21	        }
    22	        Self(outcomes)
    23	    }
    24	
    25	    fn statement(statement: &Stmt) -> u8 {
    26	        match statement {
    27	            Stmt::Return(_) => RETURNS,
    28	            Stmt::Break => BREAKS,
    29	            Stmt::Continue => CONTINUES,
    30	            Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Literal(Value::Bool(false)), .. } => RETURNS,
    31	            Stmt::Require { .. } => FALLS_THROUGH | RETURNS,
    32	            Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => Self::block(then_body).0 | Self::block(else_body).0,
    33	            // The loop may be empty or finish. Its own break/continue paths
    34	            // do not transfer out of the enclosing loop; returns still do.
    35	            Stmt::For { body, .. } => FALLS_THROUGH | (Self::block(body).0 & RETURNS),
    36	            Stmt::Const { .. } | Stmt::Let { .. } | Stmt::Assign { .. }
    37	            | Stmt::Push { .. } | Stmt::Check { .. } | Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { .. }
    38	            | Stmt::Insert { .. } | Stmt::Update { .. } | Stmt::Upsert { .. }
    39	            | Stmt::WriteTarget { .. } | Stmt::DeleteTarget { .. } | Stmt::Delete { .. } | Stmt::Emit { .. } => FALLS_THROUGH,
    40	        }
    41	    }
    42	
    43	    pub(super) fn exits(self) -> bool { self.0 & FALLS_THROUGH == 0 }
    44	    pub(super) fn falls_through(self) -> bool { self.0 & FALLS_THROUGH != 0 }
    45	    pub(super) fn terminates(self) -> bool { self.0 == RETURNS }
    46	}
    47	
    48	#[cfg(test)]
    49	mod tests {
    50	    use super::*;
    51	    use crate::bind::program::LoopDomain;
    52	
    53	    fn returned() -> Stmt { Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Int(0))) }
    54	    fn refused() -> Stmt {
    55	        Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Literal(Value::Bool(false)), code: "STOP".into(), site: None, message: None }
    56	    }
    57	    fn maybe(body: Vec<Stmt>) -> Stmt {
    58	        Stmt::If { condition: Expr::Variable("stop".into()), then_body: body, else_body: vec![] }
    59	    }
    60	    fn looped(body: Vec<Stmt>) -> Stmt {
    61	        Stmt::For { binding: "n".into(), domain: LoopDomain::LiteralRange { start: 0, end_exclusive: 2 }, body }
    62	    }
    63	
    64	    #[test]
    65	    fn transfers_and_termination_keep_their_statement_order() {
    66	        for (transfer, bit) in [(Stmt::Break, BREAKS), (Stmt::Continue, CONTINUES)] {
    67	            for end in [returned(), refused()] {
    68	                assert_eq!(Outcomes::block(&[transfer.clone(), end.clone()]), Outcomes(bit));
    69	                assert_eq!(Outcomes::block(&[end.clone(), transfer.clone()]), Outcomes(RETURNS));
    70	                let mixed = Outcomes::block(&[maybe(vec![transfer.clone()]), end.clone()]);
    71	                assert_eq!(mixed, Outcomes(bit | RETURNS));
    72	                assert!(mixed.exits());
    73	                assert!(!mixed.terminates());
    74	                assert_eq!(Outcomes::block(&[maybe(vec![end]), transfer.clone()]), mixed);
    75	            }
    76	        }
    77	    }
    78	
    79	    #[test]
    80	    fn only_falling_paths_reach_later_statements_and_loops_own_their_transfers() {
    81	        assert_eq!(Outcomes::block(&[]), Outcomes(FALLS_THROUGH));
    82	        assert_eq!(Outcomes::block(&[maybe(vec![returned()])]), Outcomes(FALLS_THROUGH | RETURNS));
    83	        let both = Stmt::If { condition: Expr::Variable("ready".into()), then_body: vec![returned()], else_body: vec![refused()] };
    84	        assert!(Outcomes::block(&[both, Stmt::Continue]).terminates());
    85	        for body in [vec![], vec![Stmt::Break], vec![Stmt::Continue]] {
    86	            assert_eq!(Outcomes::block(&[looped(body.clone())]), Outcomes(FALLS_THROUGH));
    87	            assert!(Outcomes::block(&[looped(body), returned()]).terminates());
    88	        }
    89	        assert_eq!(Outcomes::block(&[looped(vec![returned()])]), Outcomes(FALLS_THROUGH | RETURNS));
    90	        let nested = looped(vec![looped(vec![Stmt::Break]), Stmt::Continue]);
    91	        assert_eq!(Outcomes::block(&[nested, Stmt::Break]), Outcomes(BREAKS));
    92	    }
    93	}
     1	//! Conditional values keep lazy execution; incompatible row arms are refused.
     2	// @ref LLP 3000.013#7 — authoring sugar lowers to the existing ruled, metered IR.
     3	use super::*;
     4	
     5	impl Binder<'_> {
     6	    pub(super) fn conditional(&mut self, then: &A, condition: &A, otherwise: &A, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
     7	        let variant = super::super::intent_variants::condition(condition)
     8	            .map(|(kind, input)| format!("{kind}:{}", print::expression(input)));
     9	        let mut variant_body = setup::Body::default();
    10	        let output = if variant.is_some() { &mut variant_body } else { &mut *out };
    11	        let (cond, cond_ty) = self.expr(condition, output)?;
    12	        if !cond_ty.is_bool() {
    13	            return Err(type_error(condition.site(), format!("a conditional value needs a bool condition, got {}", cond_ty.name()), control::bool_condition_rewrite(&cond_ty, "x if ttl {} else y")));
    14	        }
    15	        // Each arm binds under its narrowing and runs only when taken.
    16	        let temp = self.temp("if");
    17	        if let Some(name) = &variant { self.variants.push((name.clone(), true)); }
    18	        let variant_start = self.sites;
    19	        let mut then_out = setup::Body::default();
    20	        self.push_scope();
    21	        self.narrow(condition, true);
    22	        let then_bound = self.expr(then, &mut then_out);
    23	        self.pop_scope();
    24	        let (then_expr, then_ty) = then_bound?;
    25	        let then_sites = self.sites;
    26	        if variant.is_some() { self.variants.last_mut().unwrap().1 = false; self.sites = variant_start; }
    27	        let mut else_out = setup::Body::default();
    28	        self.push_scope();
    29	        self.narrow(condition, false);
    30	        let else_bound = self.expr(otherwise, &mut else_out);
    31	        self.pop_scope();
    32	        let (else_expr, else_ty) = else_bound?;
    33	        if variant.is_some() { self.variants.pop(); self.sites = self.sites.max(then_sites); }
    34	        let ty = then_ty.join(&else_ty).map_err(|error| self.join_error(error, &then_ty, &else_ty, then, otherwise, Some(condition)))?;
    35	        then_out.push(Stmt::Assign { name: temp.clone(), value: then_expr });
    36	        else_out.push(Stmt::Assign { name: temp.clone(), value: else_expr });
    37	        output.push(Stmt::Let { name: temp.clone(), value: Expr::Literal(Value::Null) });
    38	        output.push_if(cond, then_out, else_out);
    39	        let value = if variant.is_some() { Expr::Block { body: variant_body.code, value: Box::new(ir::var(&temp)) } } else { ir::var(&temp) };
    40	        Ok((value, ty))
    41	    }
    42	}
     1	//! Job viewer scopes and calls to the same declared queries used by clients.

    13	    pub(super) fn as_viewer(&mut self, principal: &A, body: &[S], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
    14	        if self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Job || self.in_prelude || self.loops + self.caller_loops == 0 || self.viewer_depth.is_some() {
    15	            return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_CONTEXT", "as belongs inside a job sweep and cannot nest", "put as recipient.principal: inside for recipient in recipients:"));
    16	        }
    17	        let (value, ty) = self.expr(principal, out)?;
    18	        if !matches!(ty, Ty::Principal) && !matches!(principal, A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(s), .. } if snapback4_core::value::identity_fits(s) && s != snapback4_core::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL) {
    19	            return Err(diagnostic(principal.site(), "E_JOB_VIEWER_PRINCIPAL", "as requires a present non-system principal", "use a principal column or argument; keep intentional system reads outside as"));
    20	        }
    21	        let loops = self.loops;
    22	        self.loops = 0;
    23	        self.caller_loops += loops;
    24	        self.clear_authority_refinements();
    25	        self.viewer_depth = Some(self.depth);
    26	        let mut code = setup::Body::default();
    27	        let result = self.block(body, &mut code, false);
    28	        self.clear_authority_refinements();
    29	        self.viewer_depth = None;
    30	        self.loops = loops;
    31	        self.caller_loops -= loops;
    32	        result?;
    33	        out.push(Stmt::Let { name: self.temp("as"), value: Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::JobAs, args: vec![value, Expr::Block { body: code.code, value: Box::new(ir::lit(Value::Null)) }] } });
    34	        Ok(())
    35	    }

    80	        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];

   332	    fn point_event_held(&mut self, read: &Read, key: &[Value], row: &Value) -> bool {
   333	        if self.device && !crate::schema::timeline::holds_event(self.schema, &read.table, row) {

   340	    /// A key-authorized synthetic aggregate image (its fields are the caller's
   341	    /// key, the group id derived from it and the disclosed total) is sealed
   342	    /// without a second row lookup: nothing in it came from hidden storage.
   343	    pub(super) fn authorized_synthetic(&self, table: &'r crate::schema::Table, row: Value) -> AuthorizedRow<'r> {
   344	        AuthorizedRow { projection: self.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: Default::default() }
   345	    }

   347	    // @ref LLP 3000.016#2.2 — withhold missing cards before an examination cut can conceal their coverage.
   348	    // Admission still precedes candidate access (LLP 3000.015 §2): a device point
   349	    // read that cannot examine a partially held card is unknown, never a hole.
   350	    // Owned events have no horizon themselves; classify their source kinds
   351	    // from schema/key metadata even when admission prevented the lookup.
   352	    fn point_cut(&mut self, read: &Read, key: &[Value]) {
   353	        if self.device {
   354	            self.point_miss(read, key);
   355	        }
   356	    }
   357	
   358	    fn point_miss(&mut self, read: &Read, key: &[Value]) {
   359	        let table = &self.schema.tables[&read.table];
   360	        self.event_read_unknown(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
   361	        if self.device && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|s| s.horizon.is_some()) {
   362	            self.unknown_fact(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
   363	            self.components.note(|frame| frame.speculative = true);
   364	            self.withhold_unheld_card(&read.table);
   365	        } else if self.device && self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table).and_then(|m|m.row_key.as_ref()).is_some_and(|k| {
   366	                    self.schema.tables[&k.table].sync.as_ref().is_none_or(|s|s.horizon.is_some())
   367	                }) {
   368	            self.unknown_fact(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
   369	        }
   370	    }

   150	    let row = |parent, active, score| snapback4_core::value::object([
   151	        ("parentId", parent), ("active", snapback4_core::Value::Bool(active)), ("score", snapback4_core::Value::Int(score)),
   152	    ]);
   153	    assert!(predicate.matches(&row(snapback4_core::Value::Id("p".into()), false, -1)));
   154	    assert!(!predicate.matches(&row(snapback4_core::Value::Null, true, 1)));
   155	    assert!(!predicate.matches(&row(snapback4_core::Value::Id("p".into()), false, 1)));
   156	}

   159	fn maintain_identity_literals_and_scoped_rank_prefix() {
   160	    let prefix = "maintain scoped: count posts where .author = 'alice' by author, day(.at)\n  read <- allow\n  online only\n  by byCount: author, value, id\n";
   161	    let good = compile(&format!("{prefix}query scoped(c: cursor ?):\n  return scoped[viewer] after c last 10 by byCount\n"));
   162	    assert!(good.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", good.diagnostics);
   163	    let bad = compile(&format!("{prefix}query unscoped(c: cursor ?):\n  return scoped after c last 10 by byCount\n"));

   167	#[test]
   168	fn expiring_rank_rewrite_routes_by_window_intent() {
   169	    let source = SOURCE.replace("  at: time", "  until: time\n  by byUntil: until\n  expire at .until\n  at: time");
   170	    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("expiry.q".into(), format!("{source}\nmaintain totals: count posts by author\n  read <- allow\n  by byCount: value, id\n"))]);
   171	    let diagnostic = bound.diagnostics.iter().find(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_MAINTAIN_EXPIRE").unwrap();
   172	    println!("{diagnostic}");
   173	    assert_eq!(diagnostic.message, "maintain totals cannot order by value because source posts expires: stored order is stale before the physical sweep, so taking stored top-N can miss a live winner; a count upper-bound threshold scan is not built");
   174	    assert_eq!(diagnostic.guide, "feeds");
   175	    assert_eq!(diagnostic.rewrite, "for an entity-time window over a non-expiring source, use the Trending predicate in guide feeds; for expiring contributions, stream each candidate's live count and rank only a complete candidate domain; a meter cut makes the ranking incomplete");
   176	}

     1	//! What every device test needs: a chat server in memory, its pages as the
     2	//! wire sends them, and the two hosts a device runs on — the core's
     3	//! SQLite in a directory, and memory with the host keeping each commit's
     4	//! changes, the way the web does — so one suite proves both.
     5	#![allow(dead_code)]
     6	
     7	use snapback4_core::engine::{Context, Engine};
     8	use snapback4_core::error::RuntimeError;
     9	use snapback4_core::value::Value;
    10	use snapback4_device::device::{Backend, Device, StreamPage};

    14	
    15	pub const CHAT: &str = include_str!("../../../../apps/chat/snapback/schema.q");
    16	
    17	pub fn id(s: &str) -> Value {
    18	    Value::Id(s.into())
    19	}
    20	
    21	pub fn args(pairs: &[(&str, Value)]) -> BTreeMap<String, Value> {
    22	    pairs.iter().map(|(k, v)| ((*k).to_owned(), v.clone())).collect()
    23	}
    24	
    25	pub fn compile(source: &str) -> (Engine, Backend) {
    26	    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".to_owned(), source.to_owned())]);
    27	    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    28	    let engine = Engine::memory(bound.schema.clone(), bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone())).unwrap();
    29	    let backend = Backend { generation: engine.generation(), schema: bound.schema, programs: bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program).collect() };
    30	    let text = serde_json::to_string(&backend).unwrap();
    31	    let decoded: Backend = snapback4_core::json_codec::from_text(&text).unwrap();
    32	    assert_eq!(serde_json::to_value(&backend).unwrap(), serde_json::to_value(decoded).unwrap());
    33	    (engine, backend)
    34	}
    35	
    36	/// The chat server with one conversation, two members, 120 messages.
    37	pub fn server() -> (Engine, Backend) {
    38	    let (mut engine, backend) = compile(CHAT);
    39	    let mut rows = vec![("conversations".to_owned(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("c1")), ("title", Value::String("General".into())), ("creatorId", id("dev:alice"))]))];
    40	    for (n, member) in ["dev:alice", "dev:bob"].iter().enumerate() {
    41	        rows.push(("memberships".to_owned(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id(&format!("m{n}"))), ("conversationId", id("c1")), ("principal", id(member)), ("role", Value::String(if n == 0 { "admin".into() } else { "member".into() })), ("joinedAt", Value::Int(n as i64))])));
    42	    }
    43	    for n in 0..120 {
    44	        rows.push(("messages".to_owned(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id(&format!("msg{n:03}"))), ("conversationId", id("c1")), ("authorId", id(if n % 2 == 1 { "dev:alice" } else { "dev:bob" })), ("body", Value::String(format!("m{n}"))), ("at", Value::Int(1_000 + n))])));
    45	    }
    46	    engine.seed(rows).unwrap();
    47	    let backend = Backend { generation: engine.generation(), ..backend };
    48	    (engine, backend)
    49	}
    50	
    51	/// A `/sync` snapshot, as the wire sends it, from the engine.
    52	pub fn snapshot_pages(engine: &mut Engine, principal: &str, limit: usize) -> Vec<StreamPage> {
    53	    let mut pages = Vec::new();
    54	    let mut after: Option<String> = None;
    55	    loop {
    56	        let page = engine.snapshot_acquisition_page_at(principal, after.as_deref(), limit, snapback4_core::now_ms()).unwrap();
    57	        let (events, watermark, next, held) = (page.events, page.watermark, page.next.map(|next| next.encode()), page.held);
    58	        let more = next.is_some();
    59	        pages.push(serde_json::from_value(serde_json::json!({
    60	            "snapshot": true, "after": after, "held": held, "acquisitions": page.acquisitions,
    61	            "events": events.iter().map(|e| serde_json::json!({ "table": e.table, "kind": e.kind.word(), "id": e.id, "data": e.data.as_ref().map(snapback4_core::wire::to_json) })).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
    62	            "watermark": watermark, "more": more, "next": next, "generation": engine.generation(),
    63	        })).unwrap());
    64	        match next {
    65	            Some(next) => after = Some(next),
    66	            None => return pages,
    67	        }
    68	    }
    69	}
    70	
    71	pub fn stream_page(engine: &mut Engine, principal: &str, watermark: u64) -> StreamPage {
    72	    let page = engine.stream(principal, watermark, 1_000).unwrap();
    73	    serde_json::from_value(serde_json::json!({
    74	        "snapshot": false, "requested_watermark": watermark, "images_at": page.images_at, "joined": page.joined, "acquisitions": page.acquisitions,
    75	        "events": page.events.iter().map(|e| serde_json::json!({ "table": e.table, "kind": e.kind, "id": e.id, "data": e.data.as_ref().map(snapback4_core::wire::to_json) })).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
    76	        "watermark": page.watermark, "more": page.more, "generation": engine.generation(),
    77	    })).unwrap()
    78	}
    79	
    80	pub fn page_json(page: &StreamPage) -> serde_json::Value {
    81	    serde_json::to_value(page).unwrap()
    82	}
    83	
    84	pub fn context(principal: &str, pairs: &[(&str, Value)]) -> Context {
    85	    Context::new(principal, args(pairs))
    86	}

    88	/// A host: where devices live between opens. `open` gives a device on the
    89	/// named store; `close` gives it back so the next `open` finds what it kept.
    90	pub trait Host {
    91	    type Store: BoundaryMetadata;
    92	    fn open(&mut self, name: &str, backend: Option<Backend>) -> Result<Device<Self::Store>, RuntimeError>;
    93	    fn close(&mut self, name: &str, device: Device<Self::Store>);
    94	    /// Model a persisted older device format between owners.
    95	    fn patch_meta(&mut self, name: &str, changes: &[(&str, Option<String>)]);

    99	/// The core's SQLite in a temporary directory.
   100	pub struct Sqlite {
   101	    pub dir: std::path::PathBuf,
   102	}
   103	
   104	impl Sqlite {
   105	    pub fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {
   106	        static OPENED: std::sync::atomic::AtomicU64 = std::sync::atomic::AtomicU64::new(0);
   107	        let nonce = std::time::SystemTime::now().duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH).unwrap().as_nanos();
   108	        let n = OPENED.fetch_add(1, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed);
   109	        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-device-{tag}-{}-{nonce}-{n}", std::process::id()));
   110	        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
   111	        Self { dir }
   112	    }
   113	    pub fn path(&self, name: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
   114	        self.dir.join(format!("{name}.sqlite"))
   115	    }
   116	}
   117	
   118	impl Drop for Sqlite {
   119	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   120	        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.dir);
   121	    }
   122	}
   123	
   124	impl Host for Sqlite {
   125	    type Store = snapback4_core::store::Store;
   126	    fn open(&mut self, name: &str, backend: Option<Backend>) -> Result<Device<Self::Store>, RuntimeError> {
   127	        Device::open(&self.path(name), backend)
   128	    }
   129	    fn close(&mut self, _name: &str, device: Device<Self::Store>) {
   130	        drop(device);
   131	    }
   132	    fn patch_meta(&mut self, name: &str, changes: &[(&str, Option<String>)]) {
   133	        let store = snapback4_core::store::Store::open(&self.path(name)).unwrap();
   134	        let conn = store.conn();
   135	        for (key, value) in changes {
   136	            if let Some(value) = value { conn.execute("INSERT OR REPLACE INTO _meta (key,value) VALUES (?1,?2)", [key, &value.as_str()]).unwrap(); }
   137	            else { conn.execute("DELETE FROM _meta WHERE key=?1", [key]).unwrap(); }
   138	        }
   139	    }
   140	    fn label(&self) -> &'static str {
   141	        "sqlite"
   142	    }
   143	}

   145	/// Memory, with the host keeping each commit's changes as the web does:
   146	/// closing drains them into what it holds, opening hands them back.
   147	#[derive(Default)]
   148	pub struct Memory {
   149	    kept: BTreeMap<String, Vec<Change>>,
   150	}
   151	
   152	impl Memory {
   153	    pub fn new() -> Self {
   154	        Self::default()
   155	    }
   156	    /// What the host holds for one store, folded: the latest value per key.
   157	    pub fn held(&self, name: &str) -> BTreeMap<(char, String), Option<String>> {
   158	        let mut folded = BTreeMap::new();
   159	        for change in self.kept.get(name).into_iter().flatten() {
   160	            folded.insert((change.s, change.k.clone()), change.v.clone());
   161	        }
   162	        folded
   163	    }
   164	}
   165	
   166	impl Host for Memory {
   167	    type Store = MemoryStore;
   168	    fn open(&mut self, name: &str, backend: Option<Backend>) -> Result<Device<Self::Store>, RuntimeError> {
   169	        let mut store = MemoryStore::new();
   170	        store.load(self.kept.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or_default())?;
   171	        Device::open_store(store, backend)
   172	    }
   173	    fn close(&mut self, name: &str, device: Device<Self::Store>) {
   174	        self.kept.entry(name.to_owned()).or_default().extend(device.store().drain());
   175	    }
   176	    fn patch_meta(&mut self, name: &str, changes: &[(&str, Option<String>)]) {
   177	        self.kept.entry(name.to_owned()).or_default().extend(changes.iter().map(|(k, v)| Change { s: 'm', k: (*k).into(), v: v.clone() }));
   178	    }
   179	    fn label(&self) -> &'static str {
   180	        "memory"
   181	    }
   182	}
   183	
   184	/// Persist what a memory device changed so far without closing it — what
   185	/// the web host does after every call.
   186	pub fn checkpoint(host: &mut Memory, name: &str, device: &Device<MemoryStore>) {
   187	    host.kept.entry(name.to_owned()).or_default().extend(device.store().drain());
   188	}

    25	    pub(super) fn reserve_capture(&mut self, table: &Table) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    26	        let Some(through) = &table.through else {
    27	            return Ok(());
    28	        };
    29	        if self.capture_prepaid {
    30	            return Ok(());
    31	        }

    34	        self.capture_prepaid = true;
    35	        self.read_tables.insert(through.parent.clone());
    36	        self.rule_dependencies(&parent.rules.read);
    37	        self.rule_dependencies(&table.rules.insert);
    38	        Ok(())
    39	    }

    11	    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a point hit, miss or denial reserves the same reusable row scratch before discovering occupancy.
    12	    pub(super) fn aggregate_point_scratch(&self, maintain: &Maintain, key: &[Value]) -> u64 {
    13	        let source = &self.schema.tables[&maintain.source];
    14	        row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[&maintain.target])
    15	            .saturating_mul(17)
    16	            .saturating_add(
    17	                // The key is the caller's evaluated input, never a stored image.
    18	                key.iter()
    19	                    .map(|value| match value { Value::String(s) | Value::Id(s) => s.len() as u64, Value::Bytes(b) => b.len() as u64, _ => 0 })
    20	                    .fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add)
    21	                    .saturating_mul(8),
    22	            )
    23	            .saturating_add(if source.is_expiring() || (self.device && maintain.parent_key.is_some()) {
    24	                row_copy_ceiling(source).saturating_mul(32)
    25	            } else {
    26	                0
    27	            })
    28	    }

    30	    /// A maintain over expiring source rows is a live fact before the
    31	    /// physical sweep catches up. Recompute only that fact's group, with
    32	    /// the usual runtime meter and device coverage rules.
    33	    pub(crate) fn live_aggregate(
    34	        &mut self,
    35	        table_name: &str,
    36	        row: Value,
    37	    ) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
    38	        self.aggregate_source_dependency(table_name);
    39	        let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table_name) else {
    40	            return Ok(Some(row));
    41	        };
    42	        // @ref LLP 3000.015#3 — a row-keyed count certifies its live basis on the internal ledger.
    43	        if maintain.row_key.is_some() { return self.live_row_view(maintain, row); }
    44	        if self.defer_receipt_maintain(maintain) && self.receipt_maintain_pending(maintain, Some(&crate::schema::maintained_key(&row, &maintain.by)))? { return Ok(Some(row)); }
    45	        let source = self
    46	            .schema
    47	            .table(&maintain.source)
    48	            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema("missing maintain source".into()))?;
    49	        if !source.is_expiring() {
    50	            return Ok(Some(row));
    51	        }
    52	        let bytes = super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(source)
    53	            .saturating_mul(32)
    54	            .saturating_add(
    55	                super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(&self.schema.tables[table_name])
    56	                    .saturating_mul(16),
    57	            );
    58	        self.maintain_scratch(bytes, |runner| runner.live_aggregate_inner(maintain, row))
    59	    }

    61	    /// A maintained point read reserved its recount scratch up front
    62	    /// (`aggregate_point_scratch`); recompute without a second reservation.
    63	    pub(super) fn live_aggregate_prepaid(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Value) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
    64	        self.aggregate_source_dependency(table_name);
    65	        let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table_name) else { return Ok(Some(row)); };
    66	        if maintain.row_key.is_some() { return self.live_row_view(maintain, row); }
    67	        if self.defer_receipt_maintain(maintain) && self.receipt_maintain_pending(maintain, Some(&crate::schema::maintained_key(&row, &maintain.by)))? { return Ok(Some(row)); }
    68	        if !self.schema.tables[&maintain.source].is_expiring() { return Ok(Some(row)); }
    69	        self.live_aggregate_inner(maintain, row)
    70	    }

    72	    // Shared selection only: witnesses and scalar recounts keep their distinct
    73	    // authorization, coverage, lifetime and metering behavior around it.
    74	    pub(super) fn live_contribution(&mut self, maintain: &Maintain, _source: &crate::schema::Table, candidate: &Value, group: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    75	        Ok(!self.inactive(&maintain.source, candidate)?
    76	            && maintain.selected(candidate)
    77	            && maintain.source_key(candidate) == group)

    80	    pub(super) fn live_aggregate_inner(
    81	        &mut self,
    82	        maintain: &crate::schema::Maintain,
    83	        row: Value,
    84	    ) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
    85	        if self.defer_receipt_maintain(maintain) && self.receipt_maintain_pending(maintain, Some(&crate::schema::maintained_key(&row, &maintain.by)))? { return Ok(Some(row)); }

    93	            return self.backlog_aggregate(maintain, row);
    94	        }
    95	        let source = &self.schema.tables[&maintain.source];
    96	        let group = crate::schema::maintained_key(&row, &maintain.by);
    97	        let (index, prefix) = maintain.source_scan(source, &group);
    98	        if self.device
    99	            && source
   100	                .sync
   101	                .as_ref()
   102	                .is_none_or(|sync| sync.horizon.is_some())
   103	        {
   104	            self.unknown_fact(&maintain.source, index, &prefix, "live aggregate");
   105	        }
   106	        let mut bounds = Bounds::default();
   107	        let mut total = 0_i128;
   108	        let mut any = false;
   109	        'sources: loop {
   110	            let limit = 32;
   111	            // Already-authorized source candidates keep their ordinary meter; the token is the storage capability.
   112	            let rows = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| {
   113	                runner.tx.scan(allowance, &maintain.source, index, &prefix, &bounds, Dir::Asc, limit)
   114	            })?;
   115	            let count = rows.len();
   116	            for (mut candidate, key) in rows {
   117	                materialize_optional_fields(&mut candidate, source);
   118	                bounds.after = crate::key::decode(&key)
   119	                    .map(|values| values.into_iter().skip(prefix.len()).collect());
   120	                // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.6 — an expired source row is stepped over, never charged.
   121	                if self.step_inactive(&maintain.source, &candidate, &key)? { continue; }
   122	                if !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)? {
   123	                    break 'sources;
   124	                }
   125	                if !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Steps, maintain.evaluation_steps())? {
   126	                    break 'sources;
   127	                }
   128	                if !self.live_contribution(maintain, source, &candidate, &group)?
   129	                {
   130	                    continue;
   131	                }
   132	                if self.device && source.through.is_some() && self.tx.retained_row(&maintain.source, &candidate)? { continue; }
   133	                let prior = std::mem::replace(&mut self.beyond_horizon, false);
   134	                let allowed = self.provisional_readable(&maintain.source, &candidate);
   135	                let unknown = self.beyond_horizon;
   136	                self.beyond_horizon |= prior;
   137	                if !allowed? {
   138	                    if unknown {
   139	                        return Ok(None);
   140	                    }
   141	                    continue;
   142	                }
   143	                any = true;
   144	                self.note_expiry(source, &candidate);
   145	                total += match &maintain.kind {
   146	                    crate::schema::MaintainKind::Count => 1,
   147	                    crate::schema::MaintainKind::Sum { of } => {
   148	                        i128::from(candidate.field(of)?.as_int()?)
   149	                    }
   150	                };
   151	            }
   152	            if count < limit {
   153	                break;
   154	            }
   155	        }
   156	        self.meter.require_uncapped()?;
   157	        if !any {
   158	            return Ok(None);
   159	        }
   160	        if !(crate::value::I53_MIN as i128..=crate::value::I53_MAX as i128).contains(&total) {
   161	            return Err(super::maintains::aggregate_overflow(&maintain));
   162	        }
   163	        let mut fields = row.as_object()?.clone();
   164	        fields.insert("value".into(), Value::Int(total as i64));
   165	        Ok(Some(Value::Row(fields.into())))
   166	    }
   167	}

     1	# Totals: count and sum in queries, mutations, jobs and views
     2	
     3	Count rows or add up a column with `count` and `sum` inside a query, mutation or job. A view keeps a total per group current on every write, so reading it costs one row.
     4	
     5	```quarry
     6	table orders:
     7	  owner: principal
     8	  status: text <=10
     9	  cents: int
    10	  at: time default now
    11	  immutable owner, at
    12	  read <- .owner = viewer
    13	  insert <- .owner = viewer
    14	  update <- deny
    15	  delete <- deny
    16	  online only
    17	view spentByOwner: sum orders of .cents by .owner
    18	  online only
    19	view spentPerDay: sum orders of .cents by .owner, day(.at)
    20	  online only
    21	
    22	query myTotals(since: time):
    23	  open = count(orders where .owner = viewer and .status = 'open')
    24	  recent = sum(orders where .owner = viewer and .at >= since, .cents)
    25	  return { open, recent, lifetime: spentByOwner[viewer], today: spentPerDay[viewer, day(now)], yesterday: spentPerDay[viewer, day(now) - 1] }
    26	```
    27	
    28	There are two spellings, and they do not mix:
    29	
    30	- **Inside an operation** (query, mutation, job): `count(t where …)` and
    31	  `sum(t where …, .col)`. The parentheses are required; the column is the
    32	  second argument, written `.col`. `min` and `max` take the same arguments.
    33	- **In a view** (declared at top level): `view name: count t by .key` or
    34	  `view name: sum t of .col by .key`, optionally `where …` before `by`
    35	  (`sum t of .col where … by .key`, in that order). Read
    36	  it by its group key, `spentByOwner[viewer]`; a group with no rows reads `0`.
    37	  Omit `by` for one global scalar, `view orderCount: count orders`, and read it
    38	  as `orderCount`. Its authorized empty value is `0` even though zero has no
    39	  physical aggregate row.
    40	  A view over an `online only` table is read online; its own `online only`
    41	  line is optional.
    42	
    43	## In a job
    44	
    45	A job's totals are ordinary domain reads inside its sweep; write them where a
    46	screen can read them:
    47	
    48	```quarry
    49	table readings:
    50	  owner: principal
    51	  value: int
    52	  at: time default now
    53	  immutable owner, at
    54	  read <- .owner = viewer
    55	  insert <- .owner = viewer
    56	  update <- deny
    57	  delete <- deny
    58	  online only
    59	table sensors:
    60	  owner: principal
    61	  read <- .owner = viewer
    62	  insert <- .owner = viewer
    63	  update <- deny
    64	  delete <- deny
    65	  online only
    66	table dailyStats:
    67	  owner: principal
    68	  day: time
    69	  n: int
    70	  total: int
    71	  read <- .owner = viewer
    72	  insert <- deny
    73	  update <- deny
    74	  delete <- deny
    75	  online only
    76	
    77	job dailyTotals(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time):
    78	  for s in sensors by .id:
    79	    n = count(readings where .owner = s.owner and .at >= periodStart and .at < periodEnd)
    80	    total = sum(readings where .owner = s.owner and .at >= periodStart and .at < periodEnd, .value)
    81	    insert dailyStats { owner: s.owner, day: periodStart, n, total }
    82	schedule nightly every 1d at 00:05: dailyTotals
    83	```
    84	
    85	`periodStart` and `periodEnd` are the schedule's period, so each run totals
    86	one day. When the job only needs a number a screen shows, a view is usually
    87	simpler than a job: `view perDay: sum readings of .value by .owner, day(.at)`
    88	read as `perDay[viewer, day(now)]`.
    89	
    90	## Rows the caller partly reads
    91	
    92	An inline total counts only what its caller may read, so `check` refuses one
    93	whose read rule its pins do not decide (`E_QUARRY_BOUND`, or
    94	`E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT` in a mutation): it would be a different number for
    95	each caller, and a capacity check would miss hidden rows. Three exact forms:
    96	pin the deciding column (`.owner = viewer`: the caller's own total); or keep
    97	the total in a view whose read rule names who sees it (the view discloses the
    98	total, never the rows); or, in a mutation, `require` below a view's count.
    99	
   100	```quarry
   101	table staff:
   102	  principal: principal
   103	  role: text <=20
   104	  unique principal
   105	  read <- .principal = viewer
   106	  online only
   107	table requests:
   108	  owner: principal
   109	  dept: text <=20
   110	  amount: money USD
   111	  read <- .owner = viewer or exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
   112	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   113	  online only
   114	view requestsTotal: sum requests of .amount by .dept
   115	  read <- exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
   116	view requestsByOwner: sum requests of .amount by .owner, .dept
   117	  read <- .owner = viewer
   118	
   119	query deptTotal(dept: text <=20):
   120	  return requestsTotal[dept] ! else NOT_ALLOWED
   121	query myDeptTotal(dept: text <=20):
   122	  return requestsByOwner[viewer, dept]
   123	```
   124	
   125	Finance reads every department's total; each requester reads their own.
   126	`E_QUARRY_BOUND`'s rewrite prints this view for your table, column and group.
   127	A keyed private view stays nullable until its own read rule is proved. For
   128	example, after `require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
   129	else NOT_FINANCE`, `requestsTotal[dept] + 1` is well typed and a readable
   130	missing group contributes zero. A hidden stored group and a hidden miss both
   131	remain null. A following write or viewer change invalidates the proof before
   132	another keyed read.
   133	A view may omit `by` for a whole-table scalar: `view requestsCount: count
   134	requests`, read as bare `requestsCount`. The scalar still obeys its view read
   135	rule: an authorized absence is zero, while a hidden stored or absent value is
   136	null. Keep a real group key when callers need independent totals.
   137	A guard on the read rule's own grant admits an inline total, as it admits a
   138	staff page (`guide indexes`): after `require exists staff[principal = viewer,
   139	role = 'finance'] else …` (or inside `if` on it), `sum(requests, .amount)` is
   140	every row's total, because every caller who reaches it reads every row. A guard
   141	on any other role does not; use the view.
   142	
   143	For extrema over a domain, first establish that exact domain is non-empty:
   144	`n = count(orders where .owner = viewer)` followed by `require n > 0 else
   145	EMPTY`. From there, `min` and `max` of a required column on the identical
   146	domain are required values; `sum` already has a zero identity. Optional projected columns remain optional. A
   147	write, local rebinding, viewer/authority scope change, or different key/filter
   148	invalidates the proof.
   149	
   150	## Choosing
   151	
   152	- A total a screen shows on every visit: a view. It is maintained on each
   153	  write and read by key, constant work regardless of history.
   154	- A rolling window (last 7 days): bucket the view by `day(.at)` (or
   155	  `hour(.at)`, `bucket(.at, 15m)`) and add the buckets you need with point
   156	  reads, `perDay[viewer, day(now)] + perDay[viewer, day(now) - 1]`.
   157	- A one-off or filtered total (since an argument, by status): `count(...)` or
   158	  `sum(...)` in the operation. It streams the domain under the meter: each
   159	  row is authorized, filtered, folded and released. Its work grows with the
   160	  matching rows, and `check --cost` says so; bound the predicate by an indexed
   161	  time or a group key.
   162	- `count(page)` and `sum(page, .col)` over a page you already read count only
   163	  that page, not the population.
   164	
   165	## Details
   166	
   167	A meter cut never yields a partial total: the read is unavailable (the client
   168	shows `loading`), including before `??`. On a device, an inline total needs
   169	the device to hold its whole domain; otherwise the observation stays
   170	`loading`, never a partial total. Sums take `int`, `decimal` or `money` columns; a total
   171	that overflows refuses the transaction. `guide maintains` has every view form
   172	(filters, reference paths, time buckets, offsets and sync); `guide reads`
   173	covers totals beside pages and `exists`; `guide jobs` the sweep a job's totals
   174	live in.
   175	
   176	Common misspellings and what `check` says:
   177	
   178	- `sum(orders of .cents)` (the view spelling inside an operation):
   179	  `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown shape of`. Write `sum(orders where …, .cents)`.
   180	- `count orders where .owner = viewer` without parentheses:
   181	  `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown shape where`. Write `count(orders where …)`.
   182	- `sum(orders where …, cents)`: `E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN: unknown name cents`. The
   183	  column is `.cents`.
   184	
   185	Reference-grouped views over `read through .parent` rows inherit the parent's
   186	rule. Omit a read line that repeats `.parent.owner = viewer`; it is
   187	`E_REDUNDANT_RULE`, and deleting the whole line preserves the inherited audience.
   188	Maintained sums spell the value separately: `view total: sum expenses of .amount by .household`,
   189	not `sum expenses.amount by .household`.
    11	  createdAt: time default now
    12	  read <- .owner = viewer
    13	  insert <- .owner = viewer
    14	mutation createProfile(name: text 1..80):
    15	  return insert profiles { owner: viewer, name }
    16	```
    17	
    18	Reference contexts project required rows’ `.id`. Media: decimal KB/MB/GB.
    19	
    20	`?` makes columns/arguments nullable; omission is null. Table references do not
    21	read targets: use `require exists t[id] else CODE` or
    22	`r = t[id] ! else CODE`.
    23	Defaults fill omissions; explicit wins. Formats: handle/url/email; identity may
    24	be stricter than the email contract below. Money/decimal are
    25	scaled integers; fractions refuse E_INPUT. `divide` names rounding and int/int
    26	result scale. Unique-read/positive-count guards narrow; `sum` has zero. Writes,
    27	rebinding or viewer changes invalidate.
    28	
    29	## Limits
    30	
    31	- Guard or assert nullable values before passing them to required parameters: E_QUARRY_TYPE.
    32	- Values and arithmetic must fit the integer range: E_OVERFLOW.
    33	- `/` and `%` take ints and floor: `-7 / 2` is -4, `-7 % 2` is 1. A divisor must be nonzero: E_DIV_ZERO.
    34	- `divide(value, int, mode)` preserves kind; modes are `toward-zero`,
    35	  `away-from-zero`, `floor`, `ceiling`, `half-away-from-zero`, `half-even`.
    36	  Zero: E_DIV_ZERO; overflow: E_OVERFLOW.
    37	- `divide(int, int, scale, mode)` returns `decimal scale` (literal 0–12) with
    38	  checked wide scaling and an i53 result. Null is not zero.
    39	- New required defaults must also fit the existing store: E_ADOPT.
    40	
    41	## Details
    42	
    43	Types describe the values a column or argument accepts.
    44	They also set limits on text, lists and uploaded assets.
    45	
    46	### Column types
    47	
    48	```quarry
    49	table everything:
--
   100	
   101	### Int ranges
   102	
   103	`int 1..5`, `int 0..` and `int ..100` bound values; every write and call
   104	checks them (`E_INPUT`).
   105	
   106	### Text bounds
   107	
   108	`text` defaults to `text <=10000` characters in columns, arguments and helper
   109	parameters. Write `text <=N` or `text M..N` when the bound is a product rule;
   110	an indexed column must also fit the key budget (`guide indexes`).
   111	
   112	`format email` accepts one ASCII `@`. Its 1–64 character local part uses
   113	letter/digit/`!#$%&'*+-/=?^_`{|}~` dot-separated atoms; its 1–253 character
   114	domain uses 1–63 character letter/digit/hyphen labels. Dots cannot bound/repeat;
   115	hyphens cannot bound labels. `alice@localhost` is valid. Case/Unicode are not
   116	normalized; store a normalized lookup when needed.
   117	
   118	## Engine-assigned per-parent sequences
   119	
   120	`ordinal: sequence by conversationId` is a transaction-assigned `int` whose
   121	parent is a required reference. E_SEQUENCE_PARENT names a missing, optional or
   122	non-reference parent column. Ordinal and parent are immutable. Do not supply a
   123	value or default. Only `sequence by` declares an ordinal; a table named
   124	`sequence` remains a reference type.
     1	//! Search card, compiled with every other keyword card by tests/guide.rs.
     2	pub const CARD: &str = concat!(r##"# Search: word prefixes, on the server and on the device
     3	
     4	**Start here:** Declare `search` on text or enum columns, then use `matching`, order and a cursor; the planner builds the word index. This directory query uses the profiles schema below; reset the cursor when the text changes and show incomplete results honestly.
     5	
     6	```quarry
     7	query people(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
     8	  return profiles matching q after c first 20 by .handle
     9	```
    10	
    11	- `E_QUARRY_TYPE`: a search column is neither text nor enum; use one of those types before listing it in `search`.
    12	- `N_SEARCH_UNSCOPED`: authorization varies across the scan; pin the intended room, or preserve the wider domain and show partial results.
    13	- `E_CURSOR_INDEX`: an explicit index lacks a total order; use `by .column` so the planner supplies the page index.
    14	
    15	## Reference
    16	
    17	### Search one conversation, newest first, paged
    18	
    19	Use the conversation's unique index order (or equivalently `by .ordinal`):
    20	
    21	```quarry
    22	table conversations:
    23	  read <- exists members[.id, viewer]
    24	  sync to members[.id]
    25	table members:
    26	  conversation: conversations
    27	  principal: principal
    28	  unique conversation, principal
    29	  cap 50 by conversation
    30	  read <- exists members[.conversation, viewer]
    31	  sync to members[.conversation]
    32	table messages:
    33	  conversation: conversations
    34	  ordinal: sequence by conversation
    35	  body: text <=2000
    36	  search body
    37	  unique byConversationOrdinal: conversation, ordinal
    38	  read <- exists members[.conversation, viewer]
    39	  sync to members[.conversation] last 500 by byConversationOrdinal
    40	query searchChat(conversation: conversations, q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):
    41	  return messages[conversation = conversation] matching q last 20 by byConversationOrdinal after c
    42	```
    43	
    44	Both forms plan `(conversation, words(body), ordinal, id)`; the unique index still serves history and receipts.
    45	A named plain unique index supplies its order; named bracket keys must sit inside its leading prefix. Omitting keys orders by the whole index. Other plain indexes use column order.
    46	Existing integer ordinals work too: do not migrate to `sequence by` just to search (`guide sequences`).
    47	Pass `next` as `c`; reset it when the conversation or text changes. An empty needle browses in ordinal order.
    48	Keep ordinals stable while paging; refresh to include new matches before the cursor. Point decorations preserve the bare page (below).
    49	When server and offline device searches scan the same set of messages, their ordering and examined-row,
    50	index-entry, step and output-byte counts match. A device searches acquired history: show
    51	`complete: false` honestly, even for an empty result beyond its horizon; follow `next` only when present.
    52	
    53	Plain `check` counts and summarizes search warnings: unscoped completeness (`N_SEARCH_UNSCOPED`), word expansion (`N_SEARCH_WORDS`), opening refusal (`N_SEARCH_OPEN`), and per-hit roster work (`N_SEARCH_SHAPE`). `--verbose` adds explanations and rewrites; `--cost` adds execution costs.
    54	
    55	An empty needle, including whitespace or punctuation that folds to no terms, browses in the query's order under the same read rule. This directory uses one query, ordered by handle, with `next` to continue; reset the cursor when changing the search text. For a screen that wants nothing until typing, put `if q = '': return []` before the read.
    56	
    57	`search body, caption` declares the searchable columns. Enum columns use the same spelling, for example `search category`; their stored variant text is
    58	tokenized exactly like text (`'coffee-shop'` contributes `coffee` and `shop`).
    59	The planner supplies the words index: one entry per distinct word of the named text or enum columns, in that position, folded the same way everywhere
    60	(NFC, lowercase, a word boundary at every character that is not a letter
    61	or digit). The columns before it are the search's scope; the columns
    62	after it order the page and end in `id`, so a cursor continues it. A
    63	words index serves `matching` and nothing else, and cannot be unique.
    64	
    65	Indexed tokens and query terms are both truncated to 64 Unicode characters
    66	after folding (at most 256 UTF-8 bytes). Longer terms therefore search with
    67	that truncated prefix. The full composite word entry, including scope,
    68	ordering columns and appended id, must fit the portable 2048-byte index key.
    69	
    70	`matching q` folds the text the same way. Its first term selects every
    71	word it is a prefix of ("cof": coffee, coffers, cofounder — one seek each,
    72	bounded by the meter for a uniform scope; at most 32 words for an admitted
    73	non-uniform scope, whose page remains `complete: false` even below that bound), and those words
    74	merge as ordered lanes into one page — newest first with `last 20 by .at`. Every further term must be a prefix of some word of the row ("cof
    75	car" finds "COFFEE for Carol"). Mid-word text does not match; an expired row is gone at once;
    76	an edit re-tokenizes. A uniform scope (the rule decided by the room)
    77	costs one verdict per scope; with a total unique `(chatKey, principal)` key
    78	on t, `.principal = viewer or exists t[.chatKey, viewer]` is decided by the
    79	chat alone because the row is its own witness, so a roster page pinned by
    80	the chat is complete and no second table is needed. An unscoped search under a per-room rule
    81	checks the rule per row and is never `complete` (`N_NEVER_COMPLETE` names
    82	it), except for the inferred ordinary server audience described below;
    83	use the grouped or newest form below, or read on the device.
    84	
    85	The newest form below merges the viewer's rooms by `at` and `id`, with
    86	one verdict per scope when its prefix decides the rule. Its uniform lane
    87	source runs under the examined meter: `check --cost` prints `lanes ≤ meter`;
    88	an explicit `first N` above 50 still emits N_WIDE_MERGE. Use the grouped form,
    89	which pages memberships and includes each chat's hits, when opening all rooms
    90	cannot fit.
    91	A pinned conjunct such as `exists members[.roomId, viewer]` can deny a scope
    92	before word discovery even when another conjunct needs each row. Admitted
    93	non-uniform scopes keep per-row costs and remain incomplete; hidden rows
    94	there still spend the query-wide examined/index-entry budget. The admitted non-uniform scope's 32-word
    95	bound records a word cut without cutting later lanes; operator
    96	results name it in `word_cut_at`, separately from a meter cut in `capped_at`.
    97	Device reads and uniform server reads expose `cut: 'words'` or `cut: 'meter'`
    98	beside `complete`; `meter` wins if both limits cut. Non-uniform server reads
    99	report `cut: null`: their reason is withheld, with `complete: false` still truthful.
   100	`null` means "not disclosed here", not "not cut". Render "Results may be incomplete"

    27	    /// A point read: the row when it exists and the viewer may read it, else

    93	    /// The answer and the sealed price of its retained image, charged by the caller

    16	        self.read_tables.insert(m.source.clone());

    21	            self.components.note(|frame| frame.speculative = true);
    22	        }
    23	        Ok(Some(!unknown && verdict == ParentVerdict::Readable))

### 3.2 Maintained views: aggregates

```quarry
view followerCount: count follows by .followedId
view receivedLikes: count likes by .postId.authorId
view dailyPosts: count posts where not .removed by .authorId, day(.at)
view revenue: sum orders of .amount by .shopId

table posts:
  likeCount: count likes
```

These have exactly the semantics of today's maintains: the same engine, the
same recorded attribution, and maintained columns for the in-table form.
`followerCount[p]` reads the number, 0 when absent. Two defaults remove the
boilerplate lines:

- **Readability is derived.** A uniformly readable source gives a readable
  aggregate. A source whose read rule uses only bare group keys gives the same
  rule over the aggregate's key. Anything else needs an explicit `read <-`.
- **In-table columns find their reference.** A column is counted by the one
  column of the source that references the table. If there are several, use
  `count likes by .postId`.

### 3.3 Materialized views: Following

```quarry
view following: activity where .actor = viewer or exists follows[viewer, .actor]

query home(c: cursor ?):
  return following last 20 by .at after c activityCard
```

The planner recognizes this shape and materializes one inbox per reader. It
does so only when materializing discloses nothing the sources do not already
disclose. The originals, reposts and edges must be explicitly public and
fully `sync public`, with owner rules and immutable mapped columns, as the
0.3 timeline requires. Structure alone is not permission; private feeds
would need their own per-recipient authorization design.

- a planned union with an originals branch (`post: .id`, a principal actor
  column, a time column, an optional one-way removal flag);
- a reposts branch (`post:` a reference to the originals table);
- filtered by the viewer plus an edge table whose unique key is `(follower,
  followed)` of principals.

The declaration contains no sizes, no author table and no caps. Any other view
that mentions `viewer` is planned at read time.

## 4. Following without caps

The 0.3 timeline copies events inside the writer's transaction. To keep that
work under the 1,024-write mutation ceiling, it promotes authors past 50
followers to a shared broadcast lane. It merges at most 32 such lanes on
read, and it caps follows, followers, authors and events. Each of those
numbers exists only because the fan-out runs synchronously.

This RFC makes delivery engine-owned background work:
### 5.1 Streaming aggregates

`count(R)`, `sum(R, .col)` and `exists R`, where `R` is a predicate read
without a page clause, examine rows under the examined and steps meters.
Each decoded row is charged transiently, and exactly that charge is released
when the row is dropped. Retained state stays charged.

A streaming consumer keeps the underlying scan's admission, authorization,
expiry and coverage checks. Calling a scan `count` does not make it a safe
probe; LLP 3000.007 §21.9's expiring-mutation counterexample stands.
Unpaged aggregate reads retain ordinary streaming scans: membership, enum
complements and window predicates do not become bounded merge pages. Their
residual filters fold the entire admitted domain, or return null on a cut.
Streaming aggregation across planned lanes is not implemented.

The busy-chat digest claim (LLP 3000.011.001.000's 16 MiB stop) holds only
if the retained recipient-heavy fixture passes.

An inferred aggregate seek must preserve predicate short-circuiting: its
endpoint must already be present and must not fail when evaluated before the
whole predicate. Otherwise the comparison stays a residual filter. Selecting
one safe endpoint must not extract an unsafe sibling bound on the same column.
A named prefix pinning intrinsic `id` keeps its single-row access path; remaining
comparisons filter that row rather than introducing an order after the id pin.

### 5.2 Self-chunking jobs

A top-level `for x in R:` in a job, over a stable domain, is a chunked sweep.
A stable domain orders by `id` or by immutable columns; a row could otherwise
move behind the cursor. The engine commits every K iterations, with the cursor
and the evaluated domain bounds, as `chunk` does today.
### 14.7 A hidden keyed maintained value is unavailable, including at zero

A keyed maintained point authorizes the requested group before disclosing
whether storage contains it. If admitted, a stored group returns its value and
an absent group returns the maintained operation's identity, currently zero
for `count` and `sum`. If the point's read rule hides the group, a stored group
and an absent group both return `null`; the absent case must never fall through
the authorized-miss coercion and become zero. A query may instead `require` the
same authority condition first and return its chosen refusal code.

The authorization decision is identical for stored and synthetic groups even
when the aggregate's source expires. The actual system principal bypasses the view
rule for a synthetic miss just as it does for a stored hit, and therefore sees
the identity. That policy bypass does not bypass a read-through parent's
lifetime: an inherited group remains nullable until an exact live-parent point
witness proves the parent exists. A row yielded by a fresh table read can carry
that witness through a loop only when the whole loop body is proven write-free;
saved or buffered row values do not renew it, and a writeful loop needs a
current point guard in each iteration.
Because an unguarded private point can return `null`, its static result is a
nullable number. A matching effect-safe authority proof may narrow that result
to the maintained operation's required numeric type. Maintained-rule focal
fields stay scoped to the maintained target row; only declared group fields
are replaced with caller key expressions, so an unrelated caller projection
cannot supply a target-only rule field. A
write, relevant rebinding, or entry into and exit from a viewer-scoped block
invalidates that proof; a job inside `as principal` has that principal's
authority rather than the system exemption. Loop headers retain only facts
that survive every iteration, including facts on an outer binding while a
loop item shadows its source name, separately from local assignment revisions.
Program `exists` observes only rows readable by its current viewer, while an
`exists` witness inside the maintained rule tests raw presence below the
target table's read rule. A positive program witness therefore proves raw
presence, but a negative one proves raw absence only for the actual system
scope, a uniformly readable target, or a complete point key whose selected row
is structurally readable. Matching text alone does not turn a hidden miss into
a raw-absence authority proof. Authored rule constants are resolved before
group-key expressions are substituted. A parameter, local or loop item named
`allow`, `public` or `deny` remains a lexical operand rather than acquiring the
rule constant's meaning.

`check` emits `N_MAINTAIN_AUTHORIZATION` when a query directly returns a bare,
fully keyed maintained point whose read rule is narrower than the set of query
callers and no dominating `require` proves the same authority condition for
the current key and authority scope.

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation

Runtime history acknowledgment is independent of successful compiler snapshots.
First check delivers history once even on refusal; upgrade baseline checks cannot
consume it, and install-first cannot overwrite older compiler facts. Refuse an
implicit downgrade before compilation/install, with an explicit --to remedy.
Human upgrade runs human check; --json emits one object and puts progress/history
on stderr. LLP 3000.011.003.000 §11 and its packaged-proof child record this.

Dormant row populations cover their own retained cells, bytes and assets; only
active rows contribute standalone column cells/samples. The archived-row example
is one row plus two cells. Audit keeps actual restoration counters and distinguishes
a truly empty definition from zero standalone cells covered by a dormant row.
The widening note says rows that later use those values. LLP 3000.000.000 §11
and the deploy card document the disjoint counts. Validation/review follows.

     5	const SOURCE: &str = r#"
     6	table entries:
     7	  category: enum('coffee-shop', 'book_store', 'museum')
     8	  contact: text 3..254 format email
     9	  amount: decimal 2
    10	  search category
    11	  public 'wave 12 fixture'
    12	  insert <- allow
    13	  delete <- allow
    14	  online only
    15	table staff:
    16	  principal: principal
    17	  role: enum('finance')
    18	  unique principal, role
    19	  read <- .principal = viewer
    20	  online only
    21	view total: count entries
    22	  online only
    23	view categoryTotals: count entries by .category
    24	  read <- exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance']
    25	  online only
    26	mutation add(id: id, category: enum('coffee-shop', 'book_store', 'museum'), contact: text 3..254, amount: decimal 2):
    27	  return insert entries { id, category, contact, amount }
    28	mutation remove(id: entries):
    29	  delete entries[id]
    30	  return null
    31	query totalEntries():
    32	  return total
    33	query categoryTotal(category: enum('coffee-shop', 'book_store', 'museum')):
    34	  return categoryTotals[category]
    35	query guardedCategoryTotal(category: enum('coffee-shop', 'book_store', 'museum')):
    36	  require exists staff[principal = viewer, role = 'finance'] else NOT_FINANCE
    37	  return categoryTotals[category] + 1
    38	query search(q: text <=40, c: cursor ?):
    39	  return entries matching q first 20 by .id after c
    40	query extrema():
    41	  n = count(entries)
    42	  require n > 0 else EMPTY
    43	  return { low: min(entries, .amount), high: max(entries, .amount), sum: sum(entries, .amount) }
    44	query rounded(value: decimal 2):
    45	  return [divide(value, 2, 'half-even'), divide(value, 2, 'half-away-from-zero')]
    46	query average(total: int, count: int):
    47	  return divide(total, count, 2, 'half-even')
    48	query email(value: text 3..254 format email):
    49	  return value
    50	"#;

   603	        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR" && rule.effect == Effect::Update && rule.bindings.is_empty() && error.rewrite.starts_with("name both images") {
   604	            let comparison = error.message.split('`').nth(1).unwrap_or_default().to_owned();
   605	            error.rewrite = super::rule_operator::named_update(&info.decl.name, &rule.value, &comparison);
   606	        }
   607	        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP" {
   608	            error.rewrite = match rule.effect {
   609	                Effect::Read => reference::read_hop_rewrite(catalog, info, &rule.value).unwrap_or_else(|| "a read rule probes one hop: copy the deciding value onto this row (immutable, checked against the parent by the insert rule), or use read through .parent".into()),
   610	                _ => reference::reference_rewrite(catalog, info, &rule.value),
   611	            };
   612	        }
   613	        // @ref LLP 3000.036#14.3 — a read-through child binds its parent's rule over hidden
   614	        // captures (`.$enrollment.dancer`): its refusal is the parent's (`internal_names` drops the copy).
   615	        // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.6 — a `read through` that did not expand (E_READ_THROUGH says why)
   616	        // leaves its internal call; its refusal is that problem's cascade, spelled as written.
   617	        if let Expr::Call { name, args, .. } = &rule.value && name == "$readThrough" {
   618	            let reference = match args.as_slice() { [Expr::Name { name, .. }] => name.clone(), _ => "parent".into() };
   619	            error.message = format!("`read through .{reference}` did not expand, so {} has no read rule yet", info.decl.name);
   620	            // The cascade convention: `check` hides it beside its cause unless --verbose.
   621	            error.rewrite = "fix this Quarry line and rerun snapback4 check".into();
   622	            error.cascade_of = Some(rule.site.clone());
   623	            error.mechanical = false;
   624	            return error;
   625	        }
   626	        if rule.effect == Effect::Read && let Some(through) = &info.through {
   627	            error.rewrite = format!("repair {}'s read rule (its own diagnostic prints the repair); {} reads through it", through.parent, info.decl.name);
   628	            error.cascade_of = Some(error.site.clone());
   629	        }
   630	        error
   631	    })
   632	}
