   634	#[derive(Clone)]
   635	struct RuleBindings {
   636	    old: Option<String>,
   637	    next: Option<String>,
   638	    update: bool,
   639	    insert: bool,
   640	    hops: bool,
   641	    present: Vec<RuleTerm>,
   642	}
   643	
   644	// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — presence facts retain a reference term's old/next image.
   645	// Facts hold on the logical edge where the next operand can affect truth.
   646	// Expiry accounting may evaluate other edges too: the shared runtime returns
   647	// no witness for an ordinary unique null key, without accessing tied rows.
   648	// Thus admission does not require physical short-circuiting. Facts still
   649	// cannot leak from one OR alternative into an unrelated alternative.
   650	fn present_terms(rule: &RuleExpr, truth: bool) -> Vec<RuleTerm> {
   651	    match rule {
   652	        RuleExpr::Ne(left, right) if truth => non_null_term(left, right),
   653	        RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) if !truth => non_null_term(left, right),
   654	        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => present_terms(inner, !truth),
   655	        RuleExpr::And(parts) | RuleExpr::Or(parts) => {
   656	            let union = matches!(rule, RuleExpr::And(_)) == truth;
   657	            let mut facts = parts.iter().map(|part| present_terms(part, truth));
   658	            let mut result = facts.next().unwrap_or_default();
   659	            for facts in facts {
   660	                if union { for term in facts { if !result.contains(&term) { result.push(term); } } }
   661	                else { result.retain(|term| facts.contains(term)); }
   662	            }
   663	            result
   664	        }
   665	        _ => Vec::new(),
   666	    }
   667	}
   668	
   669	fn non_null_term(left: &RuleTerm, right: &RuleTerm) -> Vec<RuleTerm> {
   670	    match (left, right) {
   671	        (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Null), term) | (term, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Null)) => vec![term.clone()],
   672	        _ => Vec::new(),
   673	    }
   674	}
   675	
   676	fn rule_expr(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, expr: &Expr, bindings: &RuleBindings) -> Fallible<RuleExpr> {
   677	    let site = expr.site();
   678	    match expr {
   679	        Expr::Name { name, .. } => match name.as_str() {
   680	            "allow" => Ok(RuleExpr::Allow),
   681	            "deny" => Ok(RuleExpr::Deny),
   682	            "public" => Ok(RuleExpr::Public { reason: format!("{} is public", info.decl.name) }),
   683	            "true" => Ok(RuleExpr::Allow),
   684	            "false" => Ok(RuleExpr::Deny),
   685	            other => Err(unknown(site, format!("`{other}` is not a rule; rules are allow, deny, public, exists, created, and comparisons over ., viewer and literals"), "read <- .ownerId = viewer")),
   230	        let mut catalog = Self { ordinary_searches: Vec::new(), extremum_domains: BTreeMap::new(), canonical_read_origins: BTreeMap::new(), source, feed_views: BTreeMap::new(), read_origins: BTreeMap::new(), view_reads: BTreeMap::new(), view_types: BTreeMap::new(), documents, tables, maintains, shapes, functions, effects, synthesized: Default::default(), planner_notes: Vec::new(), membership_cap_repairs: BTreeMap::new() };
   231	        for maintain in catalog.maintains.values().filter(|m| m.read.is_none() && m.row_key.is_none()) {
   232	            // A colliding ordinary table can exist even when target generation failed.
   233	            if !catalog.tables.get(&maintain.name).is_some_and(|info| info.maintained.is_some()) { continue; }
   234	            match super::maintains::derived_read(&catalog, maintain) {
   235	                Ok(read) => catalog.tables.get_mut(&maintain.name).unwrap().decl.rules.push(read),
   236	                Err(diagnostic) => diagnostics.push(diagnostic),
   237	            }
   238	        }
   239	        catalog
   240	    }
   241	
   242	    /// The declaration site behind a schema validation path.
   243	    pub fn site_for(&self, path: &str) -> Site {
   244	        let mut parts = path.split('.');
   245	        if parts.next() == Some("tables")
   246	            && let Some(table) = parts.next()
   247	            && let Some(info) = self.tables.get(table)
   248	        {
   249	            let rest: Vec<&str> = parts.collect();
   250	            match rest.as_slice() {
   251	                ["columns", column] => {
   252	                    if let Some(c) = info.decl.columns.iter().find(|c| &c.name == column) {
   253	                        return c.site.clone();
   254	                    }
   255	                }
   256	                ["indexes", index] => {
   257	                    if let Some(i) = info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == index) {
   258	                        return i.site.clone();
   259	                    }
   260	                }
   261	                ["rules", effect] => {
   262	                    let effect = match *effect { "insert-if-absent" | "insert if absent" => "insert", effect => effect };
   263	                    if let Some(r) = info.decl.rules.iter().find(|r| r.effect.word() == effect) {
   264	                        return r.site.clone();
   265	                    }
   266	                }
   267	                ["sync"] => {
   268	                    if let Some(sync) = &info.decl.sync {
   269	                        return sync.site.clone();
   270	                    }
   271	                }
   272	                _ => {}
   273	            }
   274	            return info.decl.site.clone();
   275	        }
   276	        if path.starts_with("maintains[")
   277	            && let Some(position) = path.strip_prefix("maintains[").and_then(|p| p.split(']').next()).and_then(|p| p.parse::<usize>().ok())
   278	            && let Some(maintain) = self.maintains.values().nth(position)
   279	        {
   280	            return maintain.site.clone();
   281	        }
   282	        self.documents.first().map(|d| Site::new(&d.file, 1, 1)).unwrap_or_default()
   283	    }
   284	}
   285	
   286	fn int_range_of(ty: &crate::ast::ScalarType) -> Option<(Option<i64>, Option<i64>)> {
   287	    match &ty.kind {
   288	        crate::ast::TypeKind::IntRange { min, max } => Some((*min, *max)),
   289	        crate::ast::TypeKind::List { item, .. } => int_range_of(item),
   290	        _ => None,
   291	    }
   292	}
  1280	// @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — an insert rule may order the new row's own required
  1281	// int/time columns and integer literals: one image, no clock, no hop, no null.
  1282	// @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — decimal and money are integer minor units and order the same way.
  1283	fn insert_order(table: &Table, left: &RuleTerm, right: &RuleTerm) -> bool {
  1284	    let own = |term: &RuleTerm| match term {
  1285	        RuleTerm::Field(field) => table.columns.get(field).is_some_and(|kind| matches!(kind, ColumnType::Int | ColumnType::IntRange { .. } | ColumnType::Time | ColumnType::Decimal { .. } | ColumnType::Money { .. })),
  1286	        RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Int(_)) => true,
  1287	        _ => false,
  1288	    };
  1289	    let same = match (left, right) {
  1290	        (RuleTerm::Field(a), RuleTerm::Field(b)) => table.columns.get(a) == table.columns.get(b),
  1291	        _ => true,
  1292	    };
  1293	    own(left) && own(right) && same && !matches!((left, right), (RuleTerm::Literal(_), RuleTerm::Literal(_)))
  1294	}

  1296	fn validate_rule(schema: &Schema, table: &Table, rule: &RuleExpr, update: bool, insert: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
  1297	    let check_term = |term: &RuleTerm, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>| reference::validate_term(schema, table, term, update, path, issues);
  1298	    match rule {
  1299	        RuleExpr::Public { .. } | RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Deny => {}
  1300	        RuleExpr::Eq(l, r) | RuleExpr::Ne(l, r) | RuleExpr::Compare(_, l, r) => {
  1301	            if matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Compare(..)) && !(update && table.update_transition) && !(insert && insert_order(table, l, r)) { issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: "ordered comparisons require a named update transition, or an insert rule over the row's own required int, time, decimal or money columns (two columns of one type)".into() }); }
  1302	            check_term(l, issues);
  1303	            check_term(r, issues);
  1304	        }
  1305	        RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| validate_rule(schema, table, item, update, insert, path, issues)),
  1306	        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => validate_rule(schema, table, inner, update, insert, path, issues),
  1307	        RuleExpr::Exists { table: target, index, key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table: target, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
  1308	            if matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Created { .. }) && !insert {
  1309	                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: "created probes are valid only in insert rules".into() });
  1310	            }
  1311	            match schema.table(target).and_then(|t| t.index(index)) {
  1312	                Some(target_index) => {
  1313	                    if !target_index.is_plain() {
  1314	                        issues.push(SchemaIssue {
  1315	                            path: path.to_owned(),
  1316	                            message: format!("probe of {target}.{index} needs a plain index"),
  1317	                        });
  1318	                    }
  1319	                    if target_index.columns().len() != key.len() {
  1320	                        issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("probe of {target}.{index} has {} terms; the index has {}", key.len(), target_index.columns().len()) });
  1321	                    }
  1322	                }
  1323	                None => issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule probes unknown {target}.{index}") }),
  1324	            }
  1325	            if !equalities.is_empty() {
  1326	                if !schema.table(target).is_some_and(|table| table.index(index).is_some_and(|index| index.unique && index.columns().iter().all(|col| table.columns.get(col).is_some_and(|ty| !ty.is_optional())))) {
  1327	                    issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: "extra probe equalities need a total unique key".into() });
  1328	                }
  1329	                for (column, term) in equalities {
  1330	                    if !schema.table(target).is_some_and(|table| table.columns.contains_key(column)) {
  1331	                        issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("probe checks undeclared column {target}.{column}") });
  1332	                    }
  1333	                    check_term(term, issues);
  1334	                }
  1335	            }
  1336	            key.iter().for_each(|term| check_term(term, issues));

     3	use super::catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo};
     4	use crate::{ast::{IndexDecl, ReadExpr}, diag::Diagnostic, types::Ty};

     6	pub(super) fn candidates<'a>(info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, types: &[(Ty, bool)], synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>) -> Vec<&'a IndexDecl> {
     7	    let identity = read.index.is_none() && types.len() == 1 &&
     8	        (types[0].0.required() == Ty::Id || types[0].0.required() == Ty::Ref(read.table.clone())
     9	            || (!types[0].0.is_optional() && matches!(types[0].0.required(),Ty::Row(t)|Ty::WriteRow(t) if t==read.table)));
    10	    info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
    11	        .filter(|index| match &read.index {
    12	            Some(name) => &index.name == name,
    13	            None if identity => index.name == "byId",
    14	            None => index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len(),
    15	        })
    16	        // Explicit indexes keep their existing mismatch diagnostics and range semantics.
    17	        .filter(|index| read.index.is_some() || types.iter().zip(&index.columns).all(|((ty, literal), column)| {
    18	            compatible(ty, *literal, &column_ty(info, index, column))
    19	        })).collect()
    20	}
    21	
    22	fn column_ty(info: &TableInfo, index: &IndexDecl, column: &str) -> Ty {
    23	    if index.computed.contains_key(column) { Ty::Int }
    24	    else if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()) }
    25	    else { info.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown) }
    26	}
    27	
    28	fn compatible(ty: &Ty, literal: bool, expected: &Ty) -> bool {
    29	    ty.comparable(expected)
    30	        || (!ty.is_optional() && matches!((ty.required(), expected.required()), (Ty::Row(a) | Ty::WriteRow(a), Ty::Ref(b)) if a == b))
    31	        || (literal && expected.is_identity())
    32	}

    34	pub(super) fn refusal(
    35	    info: &TableInfo,
    36	    read: &ReadExpr,

    40	) -> Diagnostic {
    41	    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — lead a wrong-table positional lookup with the foreign reference's actual table.
    42	    if candidates.is_empty() && let Some((position, source)) = types.iter().enumerate().find_map(|(position, (ty, literal))| {
    43	        match ty.required() {
    44	            Ty::Ref(source) | Ty::Row(source) | Ty::WriteRow(source) if source != read.table
    45	                && !info.indexes.iter()
    46	                    .filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
    47	                    .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_some_and(|name| name == &index.name)
    48	                        || (read.index.is_none() && index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len()))
    49	                    .any(|index| index.columns.get(position)
    50	                        .is_some_and(|column| compatible(ty, *literal, &column_ty(info, index, column)))) => Some((position, source)),
    51	            _ => None,
    52	        }
    53	    }) {
    54	        let value = read.keys.get(position).map(|key| crate::print::expression(&key.value)).unwrap_or_else(|| "this value".into());
    55	        let mut expected = info.indexes.iter()
    56	            .filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
    57	            .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_some_and(|name| name == &index.name)
    58	                || (read.index.is_none() && index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len()))
    59	            .filter_map(|index| index.columns.get(position).map(|column| column_ty(info, index, column).name()))
    60	            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
    61	        expected.sort();
    62	        expected.dedup();
    63	        let expected = if expected.is_empty() { "a value accepted by a declared key".into() } else { expected.join(" or ") };
    64	        return Diagnostic::new(
    65	            &read.site,
    66	            "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN",
    67	            format!("`{value}` is a {source} reference, but {} key position {} expects {expected}; a reference from another table cannot select it", read.table, position + 1),
    68	            format!("replace `{value}` with {expected}, and name the intended key columns or choose its index with `by`; if this is a population read, use `{} where .<column> = <value> first 20 by .id`", read.table),
    69	        )
    70	        .guide("reads");
    71	    }
    72	    let alternatives = if candidates.is_empty() && read.index.is_some() {
    73	        info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>()
    74	    } else { candidates.to_vec() };

    76	    // text key far more often than a row id, so that named form leads (and is --fix's choice).
    77	    let mut alternatives = alternatives;
    78	    alternatives.sort_by_key(|index| index.columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id"));

    80	    for index in &alternatives {

    82	        let duplicate = info.indexes.iter().any(|other| other.name != index.name && other.unique

    85	        // Unknown explicit indexes and computed keys retain verified legacy alternatives.
    86	        changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::Index(index.name.clone()));
    87	    }
    88	    let message = if alternatives.is_empty() {

    90	    } else {
    91	        format!("{} positional argument types leave {} candidate keys; name the intended key columns or choose a compatible unique index explicitly", read.table, candidates.len())
    92	    };
    93	    let diagnostic = Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN", message, "").guide("reads").repair_read(&read.site, changes);

    95	    if read.index.is_none() && candidates.len() > 1 { diagnostic.mechanical() } else { diagnostic }

   548	/// `weaker` holds whenever `stronger` does: the same probe with fewer extra equalities.
   549	fn implied_by(weaker: &RuleExpr, stronger: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
   550	    match (weaker, stronger) {
   551	        (RuleExpr::Exists { table: a, index: ai, key: ak, equalities: ae, .. }, RuleExpr::Exists { table: b, index: bi, key: bk, equalities: be, .. }) =>
   552	            a == b && ai == bi && ak == bk && ae.iter().all(|pair| be.contains(pair)),
   553	        _ => weaker == stronger,
   554	    }
   555	}

   977	fn fields_pinned<'a>(name: &str, table: &Table, rule: &RuleExpr, pinned: &impl Fn(&str) -> Option<&'a Expr>) -> bool {
   978	    let term = |term: &RuleTerm| !matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::Reference { column: field, .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column: field, .. } if pinned(field).is_none());

   980	        RuleExpr::Or(_) => all_disjunct(rule, &|part| any_disjunct(rule, &|other| self_probe_absorbs(name, table, part, other)) || fields_pinned(name, table, part, pinned)),
   981	        RuleExpr::And(terms) => terms.iter().all(|part| fields_pinned(name, table, part, pinned)),
   982	        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => fields_pinned(name, table, inner, pinned),
   983	        RuleExpr::Eq(a, b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a, b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) && term(b),
   984	        RuleExpr::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, term)| term)).all(term),
   985	        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Deny | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => true,
   986	    }
   987	}

  1010	pub(crate) fn self_probe_absorbs(table_name: &str, table: &Table, disjunct: &RuleExpr, other: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
  1011	    let column = match disjunct {

  1280	/// The schema-derived probe allowance, independent of short-circuit answers.
  1281	pub(crate) fn rule_probes(rule: &RuleExpr) -> u64 {
  1282	    let hops = |terms: &[&RuleTerm]| terms.iter().map(|t| t.references().count() as u64).sum::<u64>();
  1283	    match rule {
  1284	        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => hops(&[a,b]),
  1285	        RuleExpr::Exists { key, equalities, .. } => 1 + key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)| t)).map(|t| hops(&[t])).sum::<u64>(),
  1286	        // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — created facts with extra equalities take the metered exists path when the fact holds.
  1287	        RuleExpr::Created { key, equalities, .. } => u64::from(!equalities.is_empty()) + key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)| t)).map(|t| hops(&[t])).sum::<u64>(),
  1288	        RuleExpr::And(terms) | RuleExpr::Or(terms) => terms.iter().map(rule_probes).fold(0, u64::saturating_add),
  1289	        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => rule_probes(inner),
  1290	        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Deny | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => 0,
  1291	    }
  1292	}

  1294	/// A rule's probe sites, as `(table, index)`: what a rule reads besides the
  1295	/// row, for live subscriptions and the partition audience.
  1296	pub fn probe_sites(rule: &RuleExpr, out: &mut Vec<(String, String)>) {
  1297	    fn term(t: &RuleTerm, out: &mut Vec<(String, String)>) {
  1298	        for (table, _) in t.references() { out.push((table.into(), "byId".into())); }
  1299	    }
  1300	    match rule {
  1301	        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => { term(a,out); term(b,out); }
  1302	        RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
  1303	            out.push((table.clone(), index.clone()));
  1304	            for t in key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)| t)) { term(t,out); }
  1305	        }
  1306	        RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| probe_sites(item, out)),
  1307	        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => probe_sites(inner, out),
  1308	        _ => {}
  1309	    }
  1310	}

  1312	#[cfg(test)]
  1313	mod lifetime_tests {
  1314	    use super::*;
  1315	    struct Witness { present: bool, until: Option<i64>, expiry: Option<i64>, visited: Vec<String> }
  1316	    impl Probe for Witness {
  1317	        fn replace_expiry(&mut self, expiry: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> { std::mem::replace(&mut self.expiry, expiry) }
  1318	        fn exists(&mut self, table: &str, _: &str, _: &[Value], _: &[(String,Value)]) -> Result<bool, crate::RuntimeError> {
  1319	            self.visited.push(table.into());
  1320	            if table == "early" { self.expiry = self.until; Ok(self.present) } else { Ok(true) }
  1321	        }
  1322	        fn created(&mut self, _: &str, _: &str, _: &[Value]) -> bool { true }
  1323	    }
  1324	    #[test]
  1325	    fn branch_selection_uses_verdicts_and_structure_including_created_tests() {
  1326	        let exists = |name: &str| RuleExpr::Exists { table: name.into(), index: "byId".into(), key: vec![], provenance: crate::ir::KeyProvenance::Const, equalities: vec![] };
  1327	        let created = RuleExpr::Created { table: "early".into(), index: "byId".into(), key: vec![], provenance: crate::ir::KeyProvenance::Const,
  1328	            equalities: vec![("kind".into(), RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Int(1)))] };
  1329	        for early in [exists("early"), created] {
  1330	            for until in [None, Some(2000)] {
  1331	                for (rule, present, verdict, visits) in [
  1332	                    (RuleExpr::Or(vec![early.clone(), exists("stable")]), true, true, vec!["early", "stable"]),
  1333	                    (RuleExpr::And(vec![early.clone(), exists("stable")]), false, false, vec!["early"]),

    95	The empty group is a real logical key, not a dummy author-visible column.
    96	Physical stores still need an index component, so the generated target carries
    97	one compiler-owned required boolean discriminator `$global = true`; its unique
    98	`byGroup` index contains only that field. Quarry never exposes the field or
    99	accepts it as a group key. Schema validation permits this exact target shape
   100	only for an ungrouped maintain; user-declared empty indexes remain invalid.
   101	Every aggregate writer uses the existing canonical maintained-row id function
   102	over the logical empty tuple, so rebuild, activation and incremental updates
   103	address the same row.
   104	
   105	The scalar name lowers to a point read of that unique discriminator. It is not a
   106	whole-table page, planned-view source, loop/aggregate domain, or `exists`
   107	target, and it consumes no arbitrary first row. Device completeness
   108	and absence use that same unique key. Consequently public synced views can
   109	prove readable absence and return zero, while private views retain null unless
   110	the same authority proof that authorizes a keyed target is live.
   111	
   112	Because the bare view name is now a scalar binding surface, an ungrouped view
   113	cannot be named `viewer`, `now`, `commit` or `new`: those names remain the
   114	contextual program builtins. Grouped views and ordinary tables retain their
   115	existing naming rules because their names are not bare scalar bindings.

   120	struct ReadProbe<'a, 'b> { runner: &'a mut Runner<'b>, allowance: &'a Authorization, table: &'a str, provisional: bool, prepaid: bool, unknown: bool }
   121	impl Probe for ReadProbe<'_, '_> {
   122	    fn take_unknown(&mut self) -> bool { std::mem::take(&mut self.unknown) }
   123	    fn take_changes(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<(i64, super::rules::Verdict)>> { self.runner.witness_changes.take() }
   124	    fn replace_expiry(&mut self, expiry: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> { self.runner.next_expiry.replace(expiry) }
   125	    fn field(&mut self, field: &str, _old: bool) {
   126	        self.runner.provisional_sequence_read |= self.provisional && self.runner.schema.tables[self.table].sequences.contains_key(field);
   127	    }
   128	    fn exists(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], equalities: &[(String, Value)]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
   129	        if !self.prepaid && !self.runner.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, 1)? { return Ok(false); }
   130	        let (present, known) = self.runner.probe_witness(self.allowance, table, index, key, equalities, true)?;
   131	        self.unknown |= !known;
   132	        Ok(present)
   133	    }
   134	    fn created(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool { self.runner.created(table, index, key) }
   135	}

    64	    fn read_authority_inner(&mut self, allowance: &Authorization, table: &str, row: &Value, prepaid: bool) -> Result<Verdict, RuntimeError> {
    65	        let provisional = self.is_provisional(table, row)?;

    76	    pub(super) fn witness_covered(&self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    77	        // Own pending writes are facts in the combined state, even in a Left
    78	        // group. Settlement removes their dependents if the server refuses.
    79	        // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — unknown coverage is not current authority.
    80	        // Until-revoked delivered images prove their own current membership;
    81	        // retained/subset images need affirmative scope evidence, not merely
    82	        // the absence of a Left marker (Unknown is distinct from Retained).
    83	        let tracked = self.schema.tables[table].leave == crate::schema::LeavePolicy::DeliveredHistory
    84	            || self.schema.maintain_for(table).is_some_and(|m| m.own_keys.is_some());
    85	        Ok(!self.device || self.is_provisional(table, row)? ||
    86	            (self.schema.tables[table].sync.is_some() && (!tracked || self.tx.current_row(table, row)?)
    87	                && !self.tx.retained_row(table, row)? && !self.tx.retained_group(table, index, key)?))
    88	    }

    90	    pub(super) fn authorization_absence_covered(&mut self, allowance: &Authorization, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    91	        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
    92	        let Some(sync) = &definition.sync else { return Ok(false) };
    93	        if definition.leave == crate::schema::LeavePolicy::DeliveredHistory && !self.tx.current_group(table, index, key)? { return Ok(false); }
    94	        if sync.horizon.is_some() || self.tx.retained_group(table, index, key)? { return Ok(false); }
    95	        let pinned: std::collections::BTreeMap<_, _> = crate::small_map::collect(definition.index(index).expect("validated index").columns().into_iter().zip(key));
    96	        // @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#5.3 — a union's absence is known through any one arm whose pinned group the viewer wholly holds.
    97	        let schema = self.schema;
    98	        let sync = schema.tables[table].sync.as_ref().expect("synced");
    99	        for (audience, _) in sync.arms() {
   100	            let covered = match audience {
   101	                Audience::Public => true,
   102	                Audience::Column(column) => pinned.get(column).is_some_and(|value| value.as_str() == Ok(self.context.viewer.as_str())),
   103	                Audience::Target { table: target, prefix, .. } => {
   104	                    let Some(mut group) = prefix.iter().map(|column| pinned.get(column).map(|value| (*value).clone())).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>() else { continue };
   105	                    let binding = crate::schema::audience_binding(self.schema, table, definition, sync).map_err(RuntimeError::Schema)?;

   112	                }
   113	            };
   114	            if covered { return Ok(true); }
   115	        }

   167	    fn witness_lifetime(&mut self, allowance: &Authorization, table: &str, row: &Value, current: bool) -> Result<(bool, bool, Option<i64>), RuntimeError> {

   173	            self.meter.heap(2 * word_heap::slots::<Value>(maintain.by.len()) + word_heap::allocation("$contribution:".len() + maintain.target.len()))?;

   175	                crate::engine::authorized_scan::InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut self.meter, |meter, scope| value.scalar_heap(scope).charge(meter, scope))?;
   176	                crate::engine::authorized_scan::InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut self.meter, |meter, scope| value.scalar_heap(scope).charge(meter, scope))?;
   177	            } }
   178	        }

   180	        let (name, prefix) = maintain.source_scan(source, &group);

   184	            [crate::schema::IndexComponent::Contribution(_), crate::schema::IndexComponent::Column(column)] if Some(column.as_str()) == source.expiry_column()));

I found a remaining global-scalar escape in schema rule probes. `exists total[true] by byGroup` goes through `schema::probe`, bypassing the program-level refusal, and can select the physical `$global` key. I’m checking the runtime witness path to confirm the effect.

NOT READY

- **P2 — Global scalars remain accessible through rule probes.** At `65ba4fe73f:snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/schema.rs:889`, `probe` lacks the global-target refusal. A rule such as `insert <- exists total[true] by byGroup` selects the generated boolean index (`schema.rs:1084`) and passes point-key admission. Runtime witnesses read raw presence (`snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/witness.rs:12`), so the rule can distinguish zero from nonzero even when `total` has `read <- deny`. This contradicts the compiler-only discriminator and no-`exists` contract. Reject global scalars in shared rule/postcondition probe admission and add refusal regressions.

The two specified round-4 counterexamples are closed in the inspected code, and the i128 wording matches implementation. Hash verified; review remained read-only, without executing tests.
